# OGFI ERP — Current Pending Implementation Plan

**As of:** August 7, 2026
**Status:** Active implementation register  
**Scope:** Production-readiness implementation remaining outside formal user UAT execution and final owner signoff

## 1. Purpose

This document is the working register for implementation that remains before OGFI ERP workspaces may be certified as production-ready. It separates shared platform work from workspace-specific completion and distinguishes required production controls from intentionally deferred future scope.

The delivery approach is:

1. Stabilize and verify the shared production foundation.
2. Complete the confirmed Inventory Control Pilot boundary in dependency order.
3. Apply the full completion gate to every workflow admitted to that pilot.
4. Keep unrelated later-phase work visible but clearly labeled as deferred from the pilot.
5. Resume broader Phase I/Phase 1.5 certification only after the urgent pilot is stable.

Formal UAT execution, evidence collection with real users, owner signoff, and the final GO / NO-GO decision remain separate release activities.

### UAT acceleration priority — July 27, 2026

The fastest safe route to Phase I/UAT readiness is to close the shared disposable-PostgreSQL evidence gap before adding higher-risk writers. The runner must use a verified ephemeral, non-production target and execute the existing concurrent race, rollback, authorization/scope, segregation-of-duties, replay, and zero-adjacent-ledger suites for completed approval, cancellation, and terminal-source fences. An unavailable or misconfigured database is a hard **NO-GO** and must not be counted as passing evidence. After this foundation is evidenced, continue with bounded non-posting source fences, then complete visible workspace surfaces and the isolated Phase I UAT/recovery gates in dependency order. Approval routing remains disabled and C4 remains dormant until its separate policy and evidence gates are satisfied.

Runner safety validation passes **15/15** lifecycle-contract tests, including target allowlisting, generated identity binding, credential scrubbing, marker attestation, and adversarial-target rejection. On July 30, an isolated detached candidate for `61578d2` used a dedicated loopback-only PostgreSQL 17 container and passed the aggregate authorization gate: 1,556 unit/manifest tests plus every database-backed authentication, access-control, adapter, administration/platform, Finance, procurement/inventory, projects/operations, workforce, route, controlled-evidence, denial, migration-ledger, adversarial-role, and seed-repeatability family. Disposable database cleanup was confirmed. This is strong local preflight evidence; hosted exact-SHA CI and signed UAT evidence remain mandatory and receive no automatic credit from the local run.

### Urgent Inventory Control Pilot priority — July 30, 2026

`DEC-0258` replaces broad workspace expansion as the immediate release priority. The confirmed target is a bounded connected Inventory Control Pilot for one warehouse, one or two branches, named users, and a selected catalog of high-risk items. The admitted control chain is organization/scope and production identity; supplier/item/UOM/location master data; Purchase Request, quotation, Purchase Order, and independent approval; receiving and discrepancy/reversal handling; immutable ledger-derived balances; transfers; blind counts and recount/correction lineage; wastage; controlled stock adjustments and reversals; evidence, audit, exception reporting/export; and hosted deployment/recovery.

Phase 1.5 Projects, Expansion, Marketing, Workforce, broad Restaurant Operations, recipe/POS consumption integration, and Finance transaction workspaces are deferred from this pilot but remain visible in navigation by explicit owner direction. They must be clearly labeled `Deferred`, `Preview`, or `Not in Inventory Pilot`, receive no current delivery effort or pilot production-readiness credit, and must not gain new authority through pilot roles. Existing server authorization remains mandatory; unsafe or incomplete actions stay disabled with an explicit reason while already-safe working behavior need not be removed. Deferring Finance delivery does not remove independent Accounting review for opening values, material or repeated loss, suspicious wastage, or stock adjustments. A variance is an investigation signal, not proof of theft; the current ledger has no ordinary sales/POS or recipe-consumption movement and those events must not be disguised as wastage or adjustments.

Delivery is split into two controlled stages:

1. **Test-data/shadow UAT:** may start on staging after named roles, pilot scope, test opening stock, approval routes, and read-only pilot-readiness checks are complete. It receives no operational stock-of-record or production-authentication credit.
2. **Operational pilot:** remains **NO-GO** until unique production accounts/MFA, exact-scope authorization, active named pilot approval routes and no-self controls, an approved immutable opening-stock cutover, count/recount correction lineage, exact-candidate database and production-authenticated desktop/mobile evidence, hosted backup/isolated restore/rollback, and signed human UAT all pass.

The dependency order is therefore: define and validate the exact pilot configuration baseline; activate only approved pilot-family routing with a usable work surface; complete the opening-stock cutover and immutable count/recount-to-correction path; close receiving, transfer, wastage, adjustment, reversal, audit, reconciliation, and exception-report browser/data-integrity gaps; close production-authenticated desktop/mobile and full local database/authorization evidence; execute local test-data UAT with `P1-UAT-011` receiving no credit until its disabled runtime is replaced by the approved lineage-safe implementation; prepare the signed release/cutover/recovery evidence locally; then wait for explicit owner authorization before any hosted deployment or supervised branch/warehouse cohort. Broader module work resumes only for a demonstrated pilot blocker or after pilot stabilization.

The requested Code Spark and exact GPT-5.4 council models were unavailable. Independent Product/Workflow, Architecture, QA, and Release reviews used the closest permitted GPT-5.6 role fallbacks and unanimously selected this boundary without relaxing any hard gate.

### DEC-0274 CI production-authenticated evidence topology — August 6, 2026

- The confirmed successor topology replaces only `DEC-0252`'s CI host-network
  implementation. Nginx owns one shared network namespace and is the sole
  host-published service at `127.0.0.1:3443`; pinned Caddy and the immutable
  exact-candidate app join it with `network_mode: service:nginx`, listening on
  unpublished loopback ports `3101` and `3102`.
- The disposable PostgreSQL service remains on a private, unpublished network.
  Its per-run nonce-bound credential is injected only into the application;
  neither proxy receives database URLs, database passwords, application/auth
  secrets, MFA seeds, or session material. Pinned separate images, read-only
  filesystems where supported, `no-new-privileges`, dropped capabilities, and
  narrowly bounded temporary storage remain mandatory.
- Acceptance requires genuine per-run Chromium CA trust plus positive and
  negative probes for the intended TLS endpoint, wrong CA, unavailable direct
  Caddy/app ports, forwarding-header replacement, startup/health/image identity,
  cancellation/failure lifecycle, complete teardown, and retained-artifact
  secret safety. TLS bypasses do not qualify.
- The current host-network Compose/runner is therefore not credited as the
  confirmed topology and must be replaced before the lane can run. A Docker
  Desktop execution is local preflight only; SPF-001/SPF-009 require the same
  complete lane to pass on hosted Linux CI for the exact candidate SHA.
  `DEC-0250`'s shared-VPS Nginx topology and every hosted deployment/recovery,
  human-UAT, and owner-signoff gate remain unchanged and open. Phase I and the
  Inventory Control Pilot remain **NO-GO**.
- The requested Code Spark and GPT-5.4-mini subagent models were unavailable;
  the confirmed Architecture/Security/DevOps/QA challenge round used GPT-5.6
  specialist fallbacks without relaxing the deliberation protocol or hard
  gates.

### DEC-0275 production-authenticated lifecycle cache and handoff correction — August 7, 2026

- Hosted run `31137408375` (candidate `e7c8da7b7ba39c10b24dcf7f9957c1ba865e52a4`) passed image construction, Playwright installation, TLS/proxy contracts, and the checks job, but both matrix lanes exited during the disposable lifecycle before browser execution. A local reproduction of the same hardened lifecycle reached all 151 migrations and exposed the first actionable failure: Vitest attempted to create `/app/packages/database/node_modules/.vite` on the lifecycle image's read-only root.
- The lifecycle Compose service now mounts bounded ephemeral tmpfs caches at the database and web workspace Vitest cache paths while retaining `read_only: true`, dropped capabilities, `no-new-privileges`, private networking, and host-UID binding. The immutable-image validator now accepts the exact CI reference form `docker.io/library/postgres:17-alpine@sha256:...`; the previous validator rejected the tag separator even though the workflow intentionally pins that form.
- The production-authenticated child command receives only the teardown stop token as an additive outer-runner environment for its authenticated cleanup command. It remains scrubbed from the runtime database environment and is not listed in the web Compose environment. A source contract and lifecycle tests cover the cache mounts, pinned image form, and cleanup-token boundary.
- Local validation: `pnpm.cmd test:production-authenticated-e2e-proxy` passed 15 tests (5 passed, 10 platform-skipped); disposable/lifecycle contracts passed 18 tests (9 platform-skipped). A hardened local lifecycle reproduction passed migrations, seed, throttle probes, and append-only guard (17/17) before reaching the next fixture-only admission check, confirming the cache and image-identity failures are corrected. A fresh hosted run on this correction is still required; Approval Inbox activation, Phase I, and the Inventory Control Pilot remain **NO-GO** until both hosted lanes complete browser, artifact-secret, teardown, recovery, and human-UAT gates.

Global routing cannot be enabled by an operator-provided Compose environment override. ### DEC-0278 production-authenticated browser startup correction — August 7, 2026

- Candidate `8958ab3b...` and hosted run `31142839350` passed the complete prerequisite checks, and both production-authenticated lanes passed disposable lifecycle startup plus teardown verification. Both browser commands then exited in approximately two seconds before producing browser evidence. Public annotations expose only exit code `1`; no browser, Approval Inbox, UAT, or release credit is claimed.
- Static inspection found the bounded Approval Worklist spec eagerly importing Prisma in the host-side Playwright process. The host runner intentionally does not receive private database credentials or private-network access; the import can fail during test collection even when the bounded test is skipped or before browser startup. The spec now creates the stale-review comment through the authorized Purchase Request UI in a separate branch-user browser context, preserving the real stale-fence behavior without host-side database access or credential exposure.
- Independent runbook review then identified three runtime blockers in that correction: the replacement context lacked the project HTTPS base URL, the login helper was not defined in the spec, and the fixture could select an approver location outside the branch requester’s live scope. The spec now supplies the authenticated login helper and trusted HTTPS base URL; fixture provisioning now selects only the intersection of active branch and approver location scopes and fails closed when none exists.
- `git diff --check` passes. Follow-up candidate `a88bbb4` (pushed to `origin/main`) contains the helper, HTTPS-context, and scope-intersection corrections and must be rerun through the exact hosted checks. The next exact-SHA hosted run must prove both desktop/mobile browser lanes, including the bounded worklist decision path, artifact secret scan, teardown receipt, and recovery checks before any Approval Inbox activation decision.
- The production-authenticated proxy contract now also guards this browser-spec boundary: no host-side Prisma client import, a local login helper, and an explicit trusted HTTPS base URL on the secondary context. This prevents the previous immediate collection/startup failure from silently recurring. The next exact-SHA hosted run remains the required evidence.

### Local-only execution directive — July 30, 2026

- The active implementation goal is to complete the Inventory Control Pilot plan step by step through all in-scope local implementation, validation, documentation, test-data UAT preparation, and release-preparation evidence. Phase I and the pilot remain **NO-GO** until every applicable identity, authorization, data-integrity, visible-surface, recovery, and human-UAT gate passes.
- No VPS pull, build, restart, migration, configuration change, staging deployment, or production deployment is authorized during this directive. New coherent slices are validated and committed locally; new pushes to `origin/main` are also held until the owner explicitly authorizes publication.
- Execution order: (1) pilot configuration baseline; (2) named approval runtime for admitted pilot families; (3) immutable opening-stock cutover; (4) stock-count recount/correction lineage; (5) receiving and transfer custody closure; (6) wastage and Stock Adjustment closure; (7) audit, exception reporting/export, notifications, and ledger reconciliation; (8) local production-authenticated desktop/mobile plus complete database/authorization/build gates; (9) local test-data UAT and signed cutover, rollback, training, and hypercare preparation; and only after explicit authorization, (10) hosted recovery/deployment evidence and the bounded operational cohort.
- Subagent model policy is distinct from the main session: the main Codex session uses an available GPT-5.6 variant; subagents prefer Code Spark and, after the Spark usage limit is reached, GPT-5.4-mini. By owner direction on July 30, 2026, if neither preferred model is selectable, the closest available permitted model may be used and must be recorded accurately. The current independent baseline re-review and Transfer/ordinary-count approval council therefore use GPT-5.6 specialist fallbacks without weakening any deliberation or hard gate.

### DEC-0259 synthetic pilot-configuration baseline — July 30, 2026

- Parent-led Workflow, Architecture, and QA deliberation plus a targeted challenge round confirmed a two-stage boundary. The local-only engineering slice now has a repository-owned synthetic manifest, stable canonical hashing, deterministic logical-ID-to-UUID derivation, and a positively attested disposable PostgreSQL fixture/validator. Every artifact is labeled non-authoritative and cannot create operational configuration, opening stock, inventory movements, UAT credit, or release authority. The fixture creates only synthetic test master/scope/permission relationships and sealed test approval-rule definitions inside the verified disposable database.
- Creating real `CompanyPolicySetting` pilot rows is deliberately deferred. After Operations/Product/Security owners confirm the real warehouse, branches, selected SKUs, named actors, count cadence, tolerances, approval routes, evidence rules, and opening values, a separate material decision must choose audited company policy versus a relational configuration model. Neither future runtime may fall back to synthetic fixture values, and configuration changes must invalidate prior digest-bound readiness evidence.
- The server-enforced relational endpoint/SKU classifier foundation, both normalized approval-family registrations, atomic transfer/count submission adapters, terminal source transitions, and visible submission/inbox routing are implemented locally default-off under `DEC-0261`; no operational configuration revision or activation row exists. The fresh disposable PostgreSQL workflow, authorization, concurrency, rollback, runtime-role, and named-eligible-approver gates now pass for these two families. Production-authenticated responsive browser evidence remains in the later exact-candidate gate. The later Phase 3 checkpoint implements the dedicated immutable opening-stock cutover; it does not turn the former ordinary opening-balance path into a valid cutover. The existing broad Phase I/Phase 1.5 minimum-count readiness script cannot certify the bounded Inventory Control Pilot because unrelated demo/project rows can satisfy it.
- Manifest validation passes **14/14** exact and adversarial contract tests. A fresh loopback-only PostgreSQL 17 run applied the exact **141-migration** ledger, seeded the isolated database, passed authentication-throttle and **17/17** append-only history probes, and provisioned/revalidated **10 actors, 10 roles, 32 exact scopes, and eight sealed approval routes**. Transactional probes rejected and rolled back extra role, scope, permission, approval-rule, and route state plus operational transfer, approval-instance, inventory-balance, inventory-movement, company-policy, notification, and audit rows. Exact zero-state assertions cover procurement, receiving, transfers, counts, wastage, adjustments, approval instances, notifications, audit, inventory, and policy. Disposable teardown left only PostgreSQL template databases, and the temporary Docker container was removed. This is synthetic baseline evidence only; it does not certify real pilot identities, policy, stock, UAT, or release readiness.
- `DEC-0259` is **complete locally**. Independent QA re-review returned **GO** with no Blocking or High findings for this synthetic baseline only. Code Spark and GPT-5.4-mini were unavailable, so the owner-authorized GPT-5.6 fallback is recorded. Repository lint and typecheck pass; the latter required a 4 GiB local Node heap after the default 2 GiB process limit was exhausted. No knowledge-base, training, release-note, or glossary behavior changed because the fixture is test-only, local-only, and inaccessible to ERP users.
- Transfer and ordinary-count approval policy is no longer open: `DEC-0260` below is confirmed. Runtime implementation and activation remain blocked by its server-enforced pilot-scope, schema, permission, producer, state-integrity, authorization, concurrency, browser, and UAT gates.

### DEC-0260 transfer and ordinary-count approval decision — July 30, 2026

- Parent-led GPT-5.6 Workflow, Architecture, and Security analysis plus a targeted challenge round confirmed mandatory normalized approval for every transfer touching the bounded pilot endpoints/SKUs and attempt-grained ordinary count review. Code Spark and GPT-5.4-mini were unavailable; the owner explicitly authorized the closest available fallback without relaxing the deliberation protocol or hard gates. `InventoryTransfer` and `StockCountAttemptReview` are the exact family/document keys; ordinary review binds `documentId` to `StockCountAttempt.id` while the UI retains the parent session, and `StockCountVarianceAdjustment` stays separate.
- `DEC-0260` is **Confirmed** and open item 20 is resolved, satisfying the implementation-lock decision prerequisite. Approval-family runtime work must begin with the blocking server-enforced pilot endpoint/SKU classifier and then preserve the dedicated transfer-approval permission, prohibited-actor separation, default-off flags, zero legacy in-flight activation, additive/forward migration, and atomic/idempotent/CAS/locked state and graph contracts. Activation and readiness remain blocked on exact database, authorization, concurrency, browser, and UAT evidence; the Inventory Pilot and Phase I remain **NO-GO**.

### DEC-0261 relational classifier and submission-intent decision — July 31, 2026

- Parent-led Architecture, Database, and Security analysis plus a targeted challenge round confirmed a normalized relational classifier rather than generic policy JSON or environment authority. The operational boundary uses immutable sealed `InventoryPilotConfigurationRevision` rows; normalized endpoint memberships with exact `TRANSFER_SOURCE`, `TRANSFER_DESTINATION`, and `COUNT_LOCATION` capabilities and exact `InventoryLocation`/parent `Location` tenant-company lineage; exact item memberships; and deterministic canonical JSON plus digest. Missing, mixed, ambiguous, cross-scope, unsealed, or digest-divergent configuration fails closed.
- `InventoryTransfer` and `StockCountAttemptReview` use separate default-off activation states backed by append-only events and CAS generations. Both active families must reference the same sealed revision; activation state/event links are acyclic; an older revision cannot be reactivated, and a content rollback requires a new higher revision. Separate environment kill switches are denial-only and never positive authority.
- `InventoryTransfer`, `StockCountAttempt`, and `StockCountSession` gain monotonically increasing versions, and every material writer must use expected-version CAS. Separate append-only typed submission intents pin the exact source/session versions and hashes, configuration revision/digest, activation event/generation, scoped idempotency key/request hash, submitter, and resulting `ApprovalInstance`. Exact replay returns the original result; any mismatch conflicts with zero mutation. The existing unique pending graph plus typed intent is authoritative, so no source `activeApprovalInstanceId` pointer is introduced initially.
- `DEC-0261` is **Confirmed**. Its additive database and default-off policy foundation is implemented locally: immutable sealed revisions and normalized memberships, append-only event-backed family activation, typed submission intents, positive source versions, exact scoped foreign keys, dedicated permissions/catalog registrations, and fail-closed classifiers exist without creating any real revision, activation, approval authority, inventory data, or readiness credit. Independent review found and the implementation corrected two Critical substrate defects before adapter work: pgcrypto calls are explicitly schema-qualified under the hardened function search path, and every activation event takes a tenant/company-wide transaction advisory lock before its family lock so concurrent families cannot commit divergent active revisions. All existing material `InventoryTransfer`, `StockCountSession`, and current `StockCountAttempt` writers now use exact version CAS and increment once on success.
- The two admitted-family producers are now local, atomic, and default-off. They classify only against the sealed active cohort; revalidate the current activation before admitting an intent; create the source transition, typed intent, graph, audit, and notification in one transaction; and do not create inventory movement or balance mutation. Feature-flag denial for an otherwise active cohort fails closed rather than silently downgrading to the legacy direct route. The visible transfer/count submission surfaces supply idempotency keys, and admitted work is routed to the normal Approval Inbox rather than a parallel pilot queue.
- Terminal handling is now local for the confirmed scope: a transfer’s final approval moves it to `REQUESTED`, while return/reject terminate the current graph and move it to the documented non-dispatchable state; an ordinary count supports final approval only and rejects return/reject. Pending transfer/count cancellation keeps the source and pending approval graph coherent in its controlled transaction. A historical approver is denied transfer dispatch and receipt custody. Exact idempotency replay remains available after a terminal decision; a returned transfer can instead use a fresh key to create its next immutable approval cycle. These are implementation facts, not activation authorization.
- Evidence on July 31: Prisma generation/validation passes; focused schema/classifier/routing coverage passes **166/166**, the approval-producer manifest passes **48/48**, the latest transfer/count source suite passes **137/137**, the stock-count guard/classifier schema suite passes **9/9**, and web TypeScript checks pass with the validated 4 GiB Node heap. A fresh isolated PostgreSQL 17 database applied the exact **143-migration** ledger (`acc56e462e8c0fecd7f56a52a5e32cc3d84a38ea0c650b6de9c43b721dead13f`), rejected ledger drift, reconciled and verified owner/runtime privileges, passed **17/17** always-enabled append-only history probes, and passed **24/24** admitted-family workflow cases. Those cases cover serial/concurrent exact replay and conflict, live permission/location revocation, self-approval, cross-tenant/company and adjacent-location forged identifiers with zero mutation, activation rollover, terminal handling, transfer custody, exact submitted-count cancellation with immutable evidence, and six late-audit rollback injections. Approval, cancellation, and failed probes created no inventory movement. The disposable database was verified and removed.
- The production runtime credential is now read-only on pilot revisions, endpoint/item memberships, activation events, and activation state; it may only read and append the two typed intent tables. The revision canonicalizer is not runtime-callable. Exact routine hashes/properties, fixed search paths, owner/runtime/PUBLIC ACLs, named trigger attachment/event semantics, and `ENABLE ALWAYS` state are attested. Direct probes also prove runtime cannot disable the stock-count history trigger and a replication-mode terminal-evidence mutation is still rejected. A token-gated Unix-socket bootstrap and rollback injector exist only inside the marked disposable runner; their post-install privileges and trigger contract are independently verified and are absent from deployment runtime.
- No real revision, activation event, cohort, approval authority, VPS/staging change, push, UAT credit, or release authority is authorized. The Inventory Control Pilot is now **66%** evidence-weighted and both it and Phase I remain **NO-GO** pending opening-stock cutover, recount/correction lineage, the remaining receiving/wastage/adjustment/report browser and integrity closures, production-authenticated responsive evidence, recovery, and human UAT. Code Spark and GPT-5.4-mini were unavailable for the material decision and independent QA/Security reviews; the owner-authorized GPT-5.6 Terra fallback was used without relaxing the deliberation protocol or hard gates. User-facing help, training, release notes, and glossary now describe only the default-off local behavior and do not announce availability.

These items must be completed once at platform level. A workspace must not be certified when it depends on an incomplete shared control.

This diagnostic contract is not completion evidence and does not change Phase I's NO-GO state.
- Restaurant-operations scan fixture correction: the diagnostic showed only Incident and Maintenance follow-ups were absent. Both services correctly apply the selected location's explicit brand scope (`brandId: session.context.brandId || null`); this test's location is company-wide while its Incident and Maintenance fixtures incorrectly set a branded ID. Those two fixtures now use `brandId: null`, matching their selected company-wide location. Branch/food scope behavior, the six-reminder `DEC-0071` contract, no-new-Food-Cost suppression, production queries, permissions, and schema remain unchanged. Exact-candidate PostgreSQL CI must still pass the repaired contract; Phase I remains NO-GO.
- Workforce exact-candidate repair: attendance-import review locked lines through obsolete column `AttendanceImportLine.batchId`; the production schema names the foreign key `attendanceImportBatchId`, so the raw lock now matches the schema and preserves tenant/company/location consistency checks before review mutation. The overtime lifecycle lock now mirrors the established leave-request contract: after tenant/company and active-location integrity checks, a caller outside the current location scope receives non-enumerating `WORKFORCE_OVERTIME_RECORD_NOT_FOUND` rather than a scope-detail error. Scope is not widened and denied actions remain no-mutation. The existing database-backed Workforce boundary tests are the acceptance contract; exact-candidate PostgreSQL CI remains required. No schema, policy, user-visible workflow, glossary, or knowledge-base update is required.
- Controlled-evidence exact-candidate fixture correction: the adjacent-location matrix correctly uses the linked Purchase Request location as the scope source for `SUPPLIER_QUOTATION`. Its fixture had instead created that Purchase Request at the caller's already-authorized home location, so the expected denial was invalid. The fixture now uses the adjacent location, preserving the production query, exact-location enforcement, no-enumeration response, and subsequent positive grant check. The same initial denial proves that a guessed controlled-evidence attachment UUID cannot bypass source-location authorization before the later exact-location grant permits download; that download boundary intentionally returns the generic non-enumerating `CONTROLLED_EVIDENCE_ATTACHMENT_NOT_AVAILABLE`, while direct source/list checks return `CONTROLLED_EVIDENCE_SOURCE_NOT_AVAILABLE`. Local disposable-PostgreSQL run `localauthz20260730evidence` passed all 19 evidence-authorization tests. Aggregate run `localauthz20260730final` then passed 1,556 unit/manifest tests, all database families (14 authentication, 6 access-control, 8 adapters, 37 admin/platform, 11 finance, 66 procurement/inventory, 19 projects/operations, 10 workforce, 6 routes, 19 evidence, and 7 denial tests), all 15 adversarial role cases, seed repeatability, 141 exact migrations, ledger drift rejection, and disposable-database teardown. Hosted exact-SHA CI remains required. No authorization policy, schema, user-visible workflow, glossary, or knowledge-base behavior changed.

## 3. Controlled Evidence Upload and Storage

Evidence capture will use a hybrid model:

- structured evidence notes;
- verified external references where policy permits them; and
- private file uploads where the workflow or risk policy requires an artifact.

Text alone must not satisfy high-risk evidence requirements unless an approved policy explicitly permits a verified external reference.

### Implemented foundation and pending activation

- The `DEC-0046` dedicated internal minimal storage broker is implemented for the same Hostinger VPS topology. Only the broker is configured to own the validated absolute private evidence bind mount and versioned AES-256-GCM key; hosted proof of that isolation remains required.
- Use separate mounts, encryption keys/versions, broker identities, scanner boundaries, credentials, and environment configuration for development, staging, and production.
- Use server-generated opaque non-repeating keys; keep tenant/company/source scope in PostgreSQL and do not encode original filenames or sensitive business data in storage keys.
- Store file metadata and source-record links in PostgreSQL; do not store file bytes in PostgreSQL.
- Enforce source-record authorization on upload, list, preview, download, archive, and replacement.
- Enforce location/department scope, confidential workforce access, and restricted-project membership where applicable.
- Validate configured size limits, allowed extensions, detected content type, and checksum.
- Upload into quarantine and prevent evidence use until malware scanning succeeds.
- Bounded upload and download streams are proxied through the authorized application and internal broker; filesystem paths, broker/scanner access, and permanent public URLs are not exposed.
- Audit upload, scan result, download, archive, replacement, denial, and retention actions.
- Preserve important evidence through archival/versioning rather than normal-user hard deletion.
- Application retention/legal-hold services and an administrative retention register, company quota reservations, upload leases/rate limits, disk high-water controls, and an evidence-root single-broker process lock are implemented. Recovery creation, isolated staging, and verification tools exist, but an independently recoverable encrypted backup, key escrow/custody, and hosted paired database/evidence restore proof remain activation blockers. Do not represent same-VPS filesystem storage as WORM or Object Lock.
- Provide one reusable attachment uploader/viewer for workspace adoption.
- Define the attachment requirement matrix by workflow, action, risk, value, and evidence purpose.
- Add cross-tenant, cross-company, cross-location, unauthorized-source, and restricted-project denial tests.

Direct web-mounted `local-private` storage remains suitable for local development and controlled demonstration only. The production design is the isolated broker contract in `DEC-0046`; confirmation of that architecture does not satisfy its implementation or activation gates.

### SPF-005 decision status — July 21, 2026

- `DEC-0046` supersedes `DEC-0045` and confirms a dedicated internal minimal broker on the same Hostinger VPS. Only the broker owns the absolute private evidence mount and versioned AES-256-GCM key; web, Caddy, and ClamAV have no evidence mount. The application proxies bounded streams, and the server issues opaque immutable keys/versions.
- PostgreSQL is authoritative for source-record authorization, idempotency, quota, quarantine, exact-version compare-and-set availability, retention/legal hold, and audit. ClamAV scans private streams through `INSTREAM`; only a conclusive `CLEAN` result for the exact version and accepted signature freshness becomes available. A bounded systemd timer handles reconciliation without detached post-response work, Redis, or a queue.
- Same-VPS filesystem storage is not WORM/Object Lock and retains root-compromise and same-disk-loss risks. SPF-005 is not production-ready. Activation remains blocked on actual VPS size/utilization and headroom, evidence quota/high-water values, encryption-key custody/recovery, Hostinger daily-backup entitlement/location/encryption/retention/restore granularity, approved RPO/RTO, retention/legal-hold policy, pinned ClamAV image/resources/signature freshness, implementation verification, and hosted paired database/evidence restore proof.
- External object storage is deferred until capacity, multi-host deployment, stronger RPO/RTO, legal WORM, or tenant scale triggers a new material decision and controlled copy-verify-cutover migration.

### SPF-005 implementation evidence — July 21, 2026

- Implemented the isolated encrypted broker and provider-neutral storage boundary, private ClamAV `INSTREAM` adapter, streamed application upload/download routes, opaque immutable key/version handling, quarantine-by-default lifecycle, and exact-version clean-release checks.
- Implemented PostgreSQL-backed idempotency, company quota reservation/consumption, upload leases, request-rate limits, bounded scan reconciliation, compare-and-set availability, legal-hold backend controls, preservation-aware workspace states, server-paginated evidence history, and the restricted administrative retention register. Authorization coverage includes upload/download, source-scope denial, legal-hold, retention-register, and non-enumerating evidence cases. Filename/MIME matching is enforced both when the upload intent is issued and before an exact version becomes available; active-script and spreadsheet extensions cannot pass as plain text.
- Added the Hostinger deployment overlay, systemd reconciliation and recovery-stage/verification units, configuration/readiness contracts, and recovery create/verify tooling. These are implementation artifacts, not proof that the real Hostinger environment, independent backup destination, encryption-key custody, or paired restore meets approved objectives.

### DEC-0098 Stock Count immutable recount recovery decision — July 24, 2026

- `DEC-0098` confirms the next implementation contract for Count Variance prerequisites: an additive immutable child attempt/version model with the existing count session retained as the stable case and attempt 1; original cutoff reuse only under continuously active movement freeze and no post-cutoff movement under the canonical inventory-location lock; otherwise a controlled close/new cutoff; explicit MFA/reason/evidence/idempotent void-for-recount for approved-but-unposted generated adjustments while preserving approval history; and full-document reversal before any posted adjustment correction. Count Variance remains inactive until the schema migration/backfill, recovery actions, authorization/segregation, notification, concurrency, blind-count, and responsive browser evidence gates pass.

### DEC-0107 My Tasks filter contract checkpoint — July 24, 2026

- Independent workflow and data/security reviews confirmed that the dashboard's required My Tasks filters cannot be shipped as client-side or post-merge filtering. The current adapters expose heterogeneous projections, source-native statuses, fixed `HIGH`/no-due semantics for most sources, no common assigned-by field, and only the already-selected location scope.
- The confirmed implementation contract is server-owned module, canonical priority, source-qualified status, selected-location context, and native `dueAt` only. Every filter must be applied inside each adapter's count and page predicate and bound into the signed cursor; `assignedBy` and arbitrary multi-location selectors remain deferred. The existing partial-source continuation risk must be resolved before filtered pagination is called complete.
- Module, canonical priority, source-qualified status, and native due-bucket filters are now live across every enrolled adapter. Filters are validated at the server boundary, intersect each source's existing authorized action predicate for both count and page reads, reject status without a selected module, use Asia/Manila day boundaries, exclude fixed/no-due sources from date reads, and bind normalized bounds into signed cursors. Assignment and arbitrary-location filters remain absent rather than inert; they require separate authority and data contracts. See `docs/core/00-governance/decisions/DEC-0107-MY-TASKS-FILTER-CONTRACT.md`.
- Focused My Tasks coverage passes 15/15; the full non-database web suite passes 1,283 tests with 301 skipped and one todo; web typecheck/lint, the 20/20 authorization manifest, isolated production build, and `git diff --check` pass. PostgreSQL boundary/count-page parity, query-plan/index evidence, responsive authenticated browser proof, and hosted gates remain open.
- Partial-source pagination now fails closed: when any selected enrolled source is unavailable, the page withholds both the total and `nextCursor`. This prevents a recovered source from being permanently skipped after a partial-page anchor; users must reload page one after source availability returns. Focused regression coverage passes for this behavior. All enrolled adapters now accept the shared module, priority, source-qualified status, and native due-bucket predicate contract; PostgreSQL boundary/count-page parity, query-plan/index evidence, responsive authenticated browser proof, and hosted gates remain open.

- The current approval-engine checkpoint uses one transaction, scoped row locks, live authority revalidation, and compare-and-set transitions. Intermediate approval keeps the source record pending and advances only the actionable step. Direct-user initial and normalized next-step assignments create at most one idempotent personal notification, while role-scoped work creates no per-user step-ready notification rows. All five initial procurement paths implement this contract, but role-scoped work has no complete actionable queue while `APPROVAL_ROUTING_V1_ENABLED=false`; the active step is authoritative, no parallel flag-off Inbox is authorized, and the existing current-user due/overdue reminder scan is only a partial non-actionable signal. Final approve, return, or reject writes one retry-safe outcome notification for the requester or responsible owner in the terminal-decision transaction. This bounded behavior does not enable broad external fanout.

Exact retries return the original draft only after live authorization plus actor, PO, location, and payload checks; changed or cross-scope reuse fails closed without returning the existing receipt ID. The UI generates a fresh key and rotates it when the selected PO changes. Focused receiving unit coverage remains green. The service retains a transaction-local raw SQL compatibility path for the new columns because the checked-in generated client must be regenerated before typed access is adopted.

Ordinary seeding is additive and idempotent, demo reset rebuilds only a loopback, positively marked disposable demo database, and database integration execution owns a uniquely named and marked disposable database. Administrative setup credentials and marker secrets are scrubbed before the application-under-test receives its per-run runtime credential.
- The Hostinger-only database role contract is implemented as packaging-neutral PostgreSQL SQL, controlled migration/verification tools, and systemd credential templates. Staging and production separate a non-login owner, controlled migrator, and membership-free runtime role. The runtime is limited to `SELECT` and `INSERT` on the three protected tables and cannot own protected objects, read `_prisma_migrations`, create schemas/databases, disable guards, alter the guard function, assume the owner/migrator role, or receive an administrative/migrator URL through the application environment. This implementation does not select between a private Hostinger host service and a dedicated private container.

## 4. Workspace Completion Sequence

After the shared baseline is stable, complete workspaces in this dependency order:

| Order | Workspace | Key completion focus | Status |
|---:|---|---|---|

Tenant-wide, unsupported future-type, and legacy named-user rules remain explicitly read-only.

PostgreSQL enforces one active slot, scoped successor/intent references, root/successor lineage, sealed immutable rule and step definitions, append-only lifecycle intents, and no rule/step deletion or truncation. - The server-derived approval and exception queue contracts now expose a bounded preview with deterministic priority ordering, location, owner, timing, and severity labels. The separate My Tasks workspace now provides exact totals and signed cross-source seek pagination for every enrolled source; totals and continuation are withheld whenever an enrolled source is unavailable. Enterprise-wide coverage remains intentionally incomplete because Approval Inbox, Count Variance, Food Cost, and sources without an approved action adapter are excluded.
- The Overview visibly labels the queue as a bounded priority preview and shows `shown of total` whenever its cap excludes matching records. Existing knowledge-base wording already describes `Today’s work` as a bounded preview, so no glossary or enablement change was required.

The test and web application typechecks/lint pass; execution remains pending because this environment does not provide `DISPOSABLE_DATABASE_ADMIN_URL` for the required seeded disposable-database E2E harness.
- `DEC-0063` confirms the path from a bounded preview to a complete cross-source queue: Overview remains a preview, while a separate server-owned `My Tasks` workspace may enroll only source-owned, authorized, seek-paginated task adapters with one tested ordering/predicate contract. Exact totals are withheld whenever a registered source is unavailable; Approval, Count Variance, and Food Cost remain excluded under their existing gates. Implementation begins with source task-contract parity, not by increasing preview limits.

This replaces the prior multi-row fixed grid that could obscure the active workspace. Web typecheck and lint pass; seeded browser execution remains pending on `DISPOSABLE_DATABASE_ADMIN_URL`. The glossary needs no change because no new user-facing term or control was introduced.
- Dashboard queue dates and due-state ranking now use the documented `Asia/Manila` operating day instead of UTC date slicing. A Manila-midnight regression test passes with the focused dashboard suite (19 tests), web typecheck, and lint. This corrects existing display/ranking behavior; it does not create a new user-facing term, workflow, or policy, so no glossary or knowledge-base edit is required.
- The first `DEC-0063` source-task adapter is now implemented for Wastage: it exposes only `SUBMITTED` reports to users with the existing review permission and `APPROVED` reports to users with the existing posting permission, with the same scoped predicate for exact count and bounded seek paging. Draft and returned reports are intentionally excluded because their current detail surface has no focused correction editor; exposing them as a task would falsely promise a completable action. Focused Wastage tests (17), web typecheck, and lint pass. This is a server-only queue foundation, so no glossary or knowledge-base update is required yet.
- Stock Adjustments now provide the second `DEC-0063` source-task adapter: only unposted `APPROVED` adjustments are returned, and only to users who already have the existing posting permission. Focused Stock Adjustment tests (18), web typecheck, and lint pass. This is a server-only queue foundation, so no glossary or knowledge-base update is required yet.
- Transfers now provide a third `DEC-0063` source-task adapter. It returns `REQUESTED` transfers only to dispatch-authorized users in the selected source location, and dispatched/partially received/disputed transfers only to receipt-authorized users in the selected destination location who are not the dispatcher. Dispute settlement remains excluded until its stricter independent-actor eligibility can be represented and parity-tested. The count and bounded seek page share this direction- and actor-aware predicate. Focused Transfer tests (22), web typecheck, and lint pass. This is a server-only queue foundation, so no glossary or knowledge-base update is required yet.
- The registered source adapters now share one deterministic cross-source cursor tuple: creation timestamp, fixed source rank, then source record ID. Same-timestamp tests prove that a later-ranked source remains eligible after a prior source page, while the current source advances only after its record ID; this prevents cross-source skips and duplicates. The first registry intentionally contains only high-priority, no-due-date task projections. Any source with different priority/due semantics must extend and test the common tuple before enrollment. Focused task-ordering and source tests (59), web typecheck, and lint pass. This internal contract introduces no user-facing term, so no glossary or knowledge-base update is required.
- The initial `My Tasks` workspace is now live in the Overview navigation. It server-merges only the enrolled requester-owned Purchase Request, Purchase Order, Transfer, Wastage, Stock Adjustment, and Receiving task adapters, reuses each authoritative service’s scoped action predicate, and opens the source record for the actual controlled action. The Purchase Order adapter returns only `DRAFT` orders to users with the existing submit permission and `APPROVED` orders to users with the existing supplier-send permission; it does not treat all open POs as tasks. Purchase Request submission now server-enforces that the draft’s recorded requester is the acting user before it creates routing, audit, or notification effects; the detail screen shows the true requester and explains the denied action. The `My Tasks` adapter returns only that user’s `DRAFT` requests with the existing submit permission. Submission on behalf remains explicitly open and is not inferred from read scope. The Receiving adapter returns only `DRAFT` receipts to users with the existing post permission; discrepancy review is excluded because it has no controlled resolution action. Its opaque HMAC-signed cursor is short-lived and bound to the current user, selected scope, permission set, and registered-source version; it cannot authorize a source action. Same-timestamp ordering remains gap-free across pages. A failed enrolled source is disclosed as unavailable and causes the total to be withheld rather than reported as zero. The page explicitly states its current source coverage, so it is an initial controlled queue rather than a claim of complete enterprise task coverage. Integration also corrected invalid dashboard-profile CSV requests so Purchase Order, Purchase Request, Transfer, Wastage, Adjustment, and Receiving exports fail closed through shared no-store validation responses instead of returning generic data. - `DEC-0071` implements the `DEC-0062` safe fallback instead of leaving known-ambiguous Food Cost signals live. Overview no longer imports or calls the Food Cost analysis, and it emits no Food Cost card, KPI, exception candidate, unavailable-source item, source-health claim, readiness badge, or aggregate contribution. Authorized recipe/cost users retain only neutral `Source workspace` navigation to the independently authorized `/recipes/analysis` route. Restaurant Operations scans no longer read Food Cost, create or refresh `FOOD_COST_EXCEPTION`, or accept recipe-only eligibility; Branch Operations, Food Safety, Incident, and Maintenance reminders remain enrolled. Existing Food Cost reminders remain stored, visible, readable, and manually archivable with a non-mutating legacy-definition warning, and they do not contribute to the page's current actionable count. Independent review exposed and drove remediation of an existing tenant-only notification boundary: list, unread count, read, and archive now share an exact tenant/recipient/selected-company and null-or-selected-location predicate, require an active selected location and a live effective company-or-location assignment, hide company-less rows, and silently deny forged or revoked-scope mutations. Notification list/count are now explicitly high-risk disclosures in the authorization manifest, and all four entrypoints have executable live-context bindings. The disposable-PostgreSQL matrix covers cross-company/location/null-company/forged/revoked cases, company-wide rows, immutable legacy Food Cost history, and two authorized idempotent scans yielding exactly the six non-Food-Cost reminders; it is implemented but locally unexecuted because `DISPOSABLE_DATABASE_ADMIN_URL` is unavailable. Final independent QA and Security re-reviews returned GO with no remaining Critical/High finding. The complete non-database web suite passes 1,216 tests, including focused 12/12 notification tests; the authorization manifest passes 20/20; typecheck, lint, and `git diff --check` pass. The preceding checkpoint production build passed, but the latest rerun stalled during Next.js compilation and was terminated without a pass/fail result. The requested Spark/GPT-5.4 models were unavailable, so GPT-5.6 reporting, workflow, UX, backend, QA, and security fallbacks were used and recorded. Workspace 1 remains in progress because a fresh production-build result, `DEC-0062` definition/reactivation work, normalized approval cutover, authenticated responsive-browser proof, disposable-PostgreSQL execution, and hosted release/recovery gates remain pending. Decision record, security/permission/reporting/UI/UAT sources, glossary, user guidance, release note, and changelog are aligned.
- The application shell no longer displays a decorative search affordance. Its notification control now opens the existing Notification Center. The dashboard adds layout-preserving loading and safe retry error states.
- Seven source services now provide dashboard-specific, server-authorized, selected-scope reads. Purchase Requests, Receiving, Transfers, Stock Counts, Wastage, and Stock Adjustments return an exact scoped count and no more than eight narrow header/exception candidates; they do not expose line, comment, audit, or evidence collections. Purchase Orders use scoped count/amount aggregates, a narrow fulfillment-line projection needed for the existing exact open/received-value calculation, and no more than eight overdue header candidates instead of materializing their related workspace records. Transfer candidates put `DISPUTED` items ahead of ordinary follow-up items before the bounded cap. The source workspace still reauthorizes every opened record.
- Branch Operations now follows the same dashboard-read boundary: Overview uses selected-scope status/exception aggregates, a critical-exception aggregate, and no more than three narrow review candidates plus three narrow exception candidates. The full checklist/line query remains confined to the authoritative Branch Operations workspace.
- Food Safety now follows the same dashboard-read boundary: Overview uses selected-scope log/reading/exception aggregates and no more than three narrow review candidates plus three narrow exception candidates. Full safety-log and reading detail remains confined to the authoritative Food Safety workspace.
- Incidents now follow the same dashboard-read boundary: Overview uses selected-scope status/severity/overdue aggregates and no more than three narrow follow-up candidates. Full incident detail and correction/evidence context remains confined to the authoritative Incident workspace.
- Maintenance now follows the same dashboard-read boundary: Overview uses selected-scope status/priority/overdue/downtime aggregates and no more than three narrow follow-up candidates. Full maintenance ticket detail, history, and operational evidence remains confined to the authoritative Maintenance workspace.
- `DEC-0062` leaves Food Cost dashboard-read replacement open. Independent reporting review found mixed default-date source data, missing ledger valuation represented as zero, and an `AWAITING_ACTUALS` definition mismatch. Do not add a bounded Food Cost dashboard projection or call the current analytical result equivalent until those source definitions are confirmed, corrected, and parity-tested; hiding the summary is the safe fallback.
- `DEC-0054` confirms the feature-disabled Approval Inbox posture. While normalized approval routing remains deliberately disabled, Overview no longer calls the unbounded legacy approval list or substitutes a direct approval query. It explicitly directs authorized users to the controlled Approval Inbox and does not represent its unavailable preview as zero assignments, a clear indicator, or an alert count. The eventual bounded approval adapter remains conditional on approved normalized-routing cutover and a minimal source-authorized projection.
- `DEC-0072` replaces eager shared dashboard-source mutation with lazy, authorized descriptors whose reads return immutable patches. Every attempted authorized source settles independently as available or unavailable with an ISO-UTC check time; unauthorized and suppressed sources are omitted. The response records assembly time, preserves a documented native `Data as of` only for sources that supply one, and never calls observation time fresh, stale, live, complete, or real-time. A deployment-configurable technical deadline defaults to 2,500 ms and rejects values above 3,000 ms; late results cannot change the returned response. A process-wide admission controller defaults to 32 and caps at 64 executing source reads, retains timed-out slots until the underlying work actually settles, and emits safe timeout/saturation/late-settlement/assembly telemetry. Composite queue contracts expose contributor provenance and make the complete total unavailable when any contributor fails, while the UI labels remaining rows as a partial view and links generically to the authoritative workspace. Trust-policy degradation fails closed for reconciliation values, and the feature-disabled Approval preview is explicitly unavailable. The dashboard Inventory Balance input is now a bounded aggregate rather than an unpaginated full-row list, with an executable disposable-PostgreSQL aggregate plus public-dashboard scope/value case registered under the procurement/inventory authorization gate. Initial Architecture and Security review found the trust, availability, telemetry, database-evidence, and unbounded-late-work gaps; final re-reviews accepted their remediation with no remaining Critical/High finding. The complete non-database web suite passes 1,233 tests, including 54 focused dashboard/inventory tests and 8 dashboard UI tests; web typecheck, lint, the 20/20 authorization manifest, isolated-output production build, and `git diff --check` pass. The PostgreSQL case is implemented but locally unexecuted until `DISPOSABLE_DATABASE_ADMIN_URL` is available. Workspace 1 remains in progress pending authenticated responsive-browser evidence, database-side cancellation/query-plan proof, staging fault injection against the four-second NFR, and the existing approval/Food Cost/hosted gates.
- `DEC-0055` confirms closed, source-owned dashboard destinations. The first activated destination is `Open POs`: the dashboard card, selected-scope count, server-paginated list, and CSV export share the exact `DRAFT`, `PENDING_APPROVAL`, `APPROVED`, `ISSUED`, `AMENDMENT_PENDING`, and `PARTIALLY_RECEIVED` lifecycle set. The destination accepts no client scope, record, status, or generic filter override; the target list, export, record view, and actions reauthorize the current session independently. Invalid dashboard-profile values do not activate a profile.
- `DEC-0056` adds the second closed, source-owned destination: `Open PRs`. Its card, selected-scope count, server-paginated list, and CSV export share the exact `DRAFT`, `PENDING_APPROVAL`, `APPROVED`, and `RETURNED` lifecycle set. The profile ignores raw status and search values, accepts no client scope or record authority, and each source operation reauthorizes the current session independently. Its user-facing wording explicitly includes approved requests.
- `DEC-0057` adds `Transfer Follow-up` as the third closed, source-owned destination. Its card, selected-scope count, server-paginated list, and CSV export share exactly `REQUESTED`, `DISPATCHED`, `PARTIALLY_RECEIVED`, and `DISPUTED` transfers where the selected location is the source or destination. The profile ignores raw tab/status/scope/filter overrides and grants no dispatch, receipt, settlement, or inventory capability; record actions independently reauthorize the current session.
- `DEC-0058` adds `Adjustment Exceptions` as a closed, source-owned destination. Its count, server-paginated list, and CSV export share exactly `PENDING_APPROVAL`, `APPROVED`, `POSTING`, and `RETURNED` records in the selected inventory location. It is read-only and preserves source authorization, no-self-approval, MFA, immutable-ledger, posting, and reversal controls.
- `DEC-0059` adds `Wastage Exceptions` as a closed, source-owned destination, sharing the selected inventory location's existing status-or-missing-required-evidence exception predicate across its count, server-paginated list, and CSV export. It remains read-only and preserves evidence policy, approval, audit, idempotent immutable posting, and reversal controls. Count Variance remains deliberately inactive: independent workflow and security review found that non-review users can currently derive frozen system quantity from the returned/displayed variance and that recount overwrites earlier evidence. Do not activate a variance KPI, queue, drilldown, or sensitive export until server-side redaction, review-only access, immutable recount history, and controlled adjustment recovery are implemented and verified.
- `DEC-0060` closes the immediate blind-count disclosure: non-review count reads and UI now redact system/variance/reviewer-derived fields, and Count Variance dashboard reads are review-permission gated. Count Variance remains inactive pending immutable recount history and controlled adjustment recovery; this security fix does not change count posting, adjustment generation, approval, or ledger behavior.
- Focused dashboard/adapter tests (including the closed PO/PR/Transfer profiles), web lint, web typecheck, the authorization-manifest gate (20), the isolated production build, release-tool self-test, and `git diff --check` pass. The refreshed authorization baseline classifies the new Branch Operations, Food Safety, Incident, and Maintenance dashboard reads as standard server-scoped workspace reads. This is not Workspace 1 completion: the Food Cost source still needs its own analytical dashboard-read design; remaining typed filtered destination contracts, database-side boundary/query-plan/concurrency evidence, authenticated desktop/tablet/mobile browser evidence, and hosted gates remain pending. My Tasks cross-source totals and pagination are complete only for the explicitly enrolled sources and fail closed on partial-source availability.
- The reusable live PostgreSQL authorization regression is registered for the new PO and PR profile reads and proves a missing read permission must deny them without mutation when it executes. It could not be run locally in this checkpoint because `DISPOSABLE_DATABASE_ADMIN_URL` is not configured; run it on the disposable/hosted gate before production readiness is claimed.
- Knowledge-base and glossary guidance now describes `Today’s work` as a read-only, scoped priority view. It does not claim that an item is complete, approved, posted, or otherwise changed merely because it appears on the overview.
- Knowledge-base and glossary impact was assessed for this internal read-path hardening. No user-visible term, navigation, permission, or workflow changed, so their existing `Today’s work` guidance remains accurate without an additional edit.

### DEC-0230 My Tasks bounded source settlement — July 26, 2026

- Every enrolled My Tasks adapter now settles at the presentation boundary within a validated technical deadline. The default is 2,500 ms and the accepted maximum is 3,000 ms. Timeout, saturation, and exception name the affected source as unavailable; healthy-source rows remain visible, while the total and continuation cursor are withheld so recovered work cannot be skipped or represented as zero.
- A process-wide admission controller defaults to 32 and caps at 64 active source reads. A timed-out read retains its slot until the underlying promise actually fulfills or rejects, and late rejection is observed. Redacted telemetry records only closed source/outcome values, timestamps/duration, and aggregate counts; it excludes users, record identifiers, query text, payloads, stack traces, and database errors. This checkpoint does not claim database cancellation or leak-free termination: permanently stuck reads can retain capacity until process/read recovery.
- The visible partial-empty state now says `No actions shown from available sources` and warns that it is not confirmation no work is waiting. Complete empty pages retain the positive all-clear state. Task eligibility, ordering, cursor binding, permissions, scope, source action authority, and the underlying workflow services are unchanged.
- Focused My Tasks service and visible-state coverage passes 25/25; the authorization manifest passes 20/20; the complete non-database web suite passes 1,395 tests with 305 skipped and one existing TODO across 127 passed/11 skipped files. Web typecheck and lint, E2E typecheck, production build, secret review, and `git diff --check` pass. The authenticated disposable-database E2E entry point was attempted and failed closed before database creation with `DISPOSABLE_DATABASE_ADMIN_URL_REQUIRED`; no browser or PostgreSQL execution credit is claimed.
- The source-of-truth dashboard UI specification, knowledge-base guide, decision record, and user-facing release note are aligned. No glossary term, permission document, data dictionary, migration, or training-content change is required because this correction adds no user term, authority, field, status, or workflow policy. Requested Code Spark and exact GPT-5.4 specialists were unavailable, so the closest permitted GPT-5.6 specialist fallbacks were used without relaxing gates.
- Independent final Security/QA and Product/UX reviews both returned source-checkpoint GO with no Critical, High, or Medium finding. Workspace 1 and Phase I remain **NO-GO** pending hosted load/threshold/alert/recovery evidence, database statement-timeout or cancellation posture, authenticated desktop/tablet/mobile evidence, production-authenticated E2E, UAT, and the existing normalized-approval, Food Cost, Count Variance, PostgreSQL query-plan/concurrency, deployment, and recovery gates.

### DEC-0239 Master Data Item Category/UOM lifecycle locks — July 26, 2026

- The Master Data production-readiness review is in progress. `DEC-0239` closes the local source race in which Item creation and concurrent Item Category or UOM deactivation could independently validate and both commit. Creation now locks and revalidates the exact scoped Category followed by sorted distinct base/purchase/issue UOMs inside the transaction. Category and UOM deactivation lock the same scoped parent before counting active dependent Items and changing status. The serialized contract permits only one safe ordering: creation wins and deactivation rejects its active dependent, or deactivation wins and creation rejects the inactive parent. `DEC-0241` later narrowed current Item editing to name-only correction, so material edits do not participate in this parent lifecycle matrix. Existing tenant/company authorization, duplicate/base-UOM-history controls for future governed edits, lifecycle states, stable errors, atomic audit, and no-hard-delete behavior remain unchanged.
- Parent-led deliberation began with Product favoring the greater-than-100-option composer, UX favoring a focused TaskSheet, and Security identifying the lifecycle race as Blocking. The challenge round applied the data-integrity hard gate and reached consensus on the race-first correction. The option composer and TaskSheet remain separate visible-surface completion work. No schema, migration, permission, workflow, inventory, purchasing, reporting, export, or user-authority change is introduced.
- Focused Item coverage passes 10/10; the complete non-database web suite passes 1,424 tests with 305 skipped and one existing TODO across 129 passed/11 skipped files. Web typecheck/lint, E2E typecheck, production build, the 20/20 authorization manifest, secret review, and `git diff --check` pass. Final Product, Security, and UX reviews each return **GO** C0/H0/M0/L0 for this local source checkpoint. The current executable disposable-PostgreSQL matrix is authored and registered in the procurement/inventory runner: four tests cover Item creation against Category and base/purchase/issue UOM deactivation, and each test runs both winner orders for eight races. The matrix requires distinct backend PIDs, observes real waits through `pg_blocking_pids`, checks stable loser errors, and asserts the final invariant plus atomic source/audit outcomes. It is discovered and skips safely without the integration sentinel. The runner was attempted but still fails closed before database creation with `DISPOSABLE_DATABASE_ADMIN_URL_REQUIRED`, so no PostgreSQL or two-connection race execution credit is claimed. Authenticated responsive browser, hosted recovery/deployment, and UAT remain open. Master Data and Phase I remain incomplete and **NO-GO**. Requested Code Spark and exact GPT-5.4 reviewers were unavailable; the closest permitted GPT-5.6 specialists were used without relaxing any gate.

## 5. Workspace Completion Gate

Every workspace must satisfy all applicable items before it is marked complete:

- All visible tabs, panels, actions, and navigation destinations work or clearly explain their intentional read-only/disabled state.
- Create, edit, submit, approve, post, receive, dispatch, complete, cancel, archive, reverse, export, and configuration actions work where the UI implies them.
- Server-side tenant and scope authorization is enforced; UI hiding is not treated as security.
- Valid transitions succeed and invalid transitions are rejected with stable, user-safe errors.
- Financial and inventory segregation-of-duties rules, including no self-approval, are enforced.
- Retried posting actions are idempotent and concurrency-sensitive actions detect stale versions.
- Important changes preserve audit/activity history and do not hard-delete controlled records.
- Required reasons, evidence, attachments, and independent review are policy-driven and enforced.
- Lists provide the required search, filters, pagination, export, and mobile presentation.
- Empty, loading, error, denied, returned, rejected, conflict, and retry states are usable.
- Desktop, tablet, and mobile task flows are verified.
- Relevant unit, integration, access-control, and focused E2E tests pass.
- Workflow, permission, data, UI, knowledge-base, release-note, and training documentation impacts are assessed and updated where behavior changed.

## 6. Phase I and Phase 1.5 Deferred Controlled Transitions

The following remain pending implementation or require a separate confirmed release slice. They must not be represented as available merely because adjacent foundations exist:

- full post-receiving PO amendment for supplier, location, lines, substitutions, and payment terms;
- partial receiving-line reversal;
- transfer dispatch reversal and automated replacement/financial settlement;
- backdated operational corrections outside approved controlled paths;
- opening-balance and inventory cutover execution after the final cutover policy is confirmed;
- automated notification scheduling and external email/chat/SMS delivery;
- time-limited attachment links where direct signed URLs are adopted;
- formal PDF summaries where required;
- broader task dependency enforcement, drag/drop concurrency, and automation beyond the approved Phase 1.5 boundary.
- Phase 1.5 Project Board still has implementation blockers before its workspace gate: the visible board needs the documented project context header, linked-record/assignee indicators, complete status controls, mobile filtered-list mode, and server pagination; task-key allocation must replace the concurrent `count + 1` pattern; project/task dates must render in the company or user timezone; and the enabled task-transition policy needs an explicit lifecycle matrix. These are recorded for the later Projects & Implementation Tracker workspace and must not be hidden behind the current Phase I stabilization work.
- The same Phase 1.5 audit found that `/projects` reports all active members and open risks but renders only `members.slice(0, 10)` and `risks.slice(0, 8)` from unbounded service reads. Replace those truncations with server-owned, scoped pagination and truthful page/empty/error states before the project workspace can pass SPF-008.
- Administration remains the next Phase I workspace after Overview stabilization. Its previously identified unbounded/hard-capped users, roles, organization, audit, request, and permission reads have received bounded URL-backed contracts and focused visible-state hardening through the DEC-0162–DEC-0218 checkpoints. Administration is still not complete: disposable PostgreSQL no-query/isolation/query-plan evidence, responsive browser/mobile verification, hosted recovery/deployment, production-authenticated E2E, and UAT remain open, and no server guard may be relaxed.

## 7. Later-Phase Boundaries

### Phase 2 — Restaurant Operations and Food Cost

Pending production work includes full regression, mobile operational verification, trusted sales/POS source validation, attachment adoption, training impact, and resolution of any formula or workflow defects found during the workspace pass. Recursive sub-recipe flattening and bulk mutation remain deferred unless separately approved.

### Phase 3 — Finance and Workforce

The current implementation is a controlled foundation. Production implementation remains pending for supplier-credit application, AP settlement, payment execution, bank effects/integration, official accounting consequences, cash-advance and petty-cash settlement, budget hard-block rollout/backfill, broader reconciliation exceptions, production close resolution, full workforce transfer approval, and production document retention.

Phase 3 must not be presented as the official accounting book of record until these controls and the required finance-owner validations are complete.

### Phase 4 — Expansion Projects

The workspace foundation remains subject to the shared attachment/storage, authorization, release-evidence, and workspace completion gates. Contractor portals, public links, advanced Gantt calculations, workload balancing, and custom automation builders remain intentionally out of scope unless separately approved.

## 8. Production Configuration Still Required

- Approval thresholds and authorized approvers
- Emergency-purchase ceiling and post-review deadline
- Evidence and attachment requirements by workflow, action, value, and risk
- Lot/expiry-controlled categories
- Stock-count frequency by location/category
- Opening inventory date and valuation method
- Supplier accreditation and blocked-supplier rules
- Retention, privacy, backup, and restore ownership
- Production evidence-storage provider, limits, scanning, and recovery policy
- Monitoring, incident response, and hypercare ownership

### Disposable PostgreSQL custody and authorization gate — July 31, 2026

- The fresh disposable PostgreSQL harness completed the full 148-migration ledger/role-contract preflight, seed, authentication-throttle control/runtime probes, append-only database tests **17/17**, and the procurement/inventory authorization acceptance lane **77/77 across 9 files**. The database and uniquely named roles were created for the run and removed by the harness; no VPS or persistent local application database was changed.
- The first run exposed that PostgreSQL rejects `FOR UPDATE`/`FOR SHARE` row locks on the append-only `InventoryMovement` table for the runtime role. Granting UPDATE would violate the reviewed role contract. The reversal paths now acquire transaction-scoped advisory locks keyed by each immutable original movement ID, return only supported scalar columns to Prisma, and retain the source-row claim as the terminal idempotency serialization point. The follow-up custody race suite passed **2/2**, and the full lane passed **77/77**.
- A targeted challenge review accepted the advisory-lock choice as serious-but-manageable: the source-row compare-and-swap and unique lineage constraints remain the authoritative duplicate-reversal controls; movement-ID advisory keys are acquired in deterministic ID order before provenance/counter posting. Future original-movement recovery writers must use the same convention, and any new multi-movement writer requires an explicit competing-transaction/rollback test.
- This closes the local disposable database/authorization gate for the current custody and export-adjacent implementation slice. It does not close production-authenticated browser evidence, the remaining responsive Expansion timeout investigation, recovery/deployment authority, external UAT, or owner signoff. Inventory Control Pilot and Phase I remain **NO-GO**.
- Final post-hardening validation passed: web typecheck (including route-type generation), web lint, and `git diff --check` all exit 0. No commit or push was performed.
- A local production-authenticated browser attempt was made with the CI-equivalent per-run CA, host-mounted TLS material, production runtime flags, and disposable database target. The attempt reached the Playwright installation prerequisite but the nested Debian package installation stalled and was terminated; no browser test started, no fixture/credential was retained, and no production-authenticated browser credit is claimed. The CI-only lane remains the authoritative path until a reproducible local browser dependency environment is available without weakening TLS or fixture gates.

The existing `if: always()` evidence upload and read-only workflow permissions remain unchanged. A fresh exact-SHA hosted run is required to identify and then close the ordinary E2E failure. Phase I remains **77% local implementation / NO-GO**, Inventory Control Pilot remains **89% local implementation / NO-GO**, and no VPS action is authorized or taken.
