### Hosted topology validator null-field correction — August 7, 2026

- The downloaded log archive for run `31172250975` confirms both browser lanes passed private-image admission and started the isolated database lifecycle. Playwright then reported `[WebServer] Cannot read properties of null (reading 'map')` followed by `Process from config.webServer was not able to start`; cleanup completed, but no browser test executed and no browser/UAT/release credit is claimed.
- The exact cause is in the runner’s Docker topology inspection: Docker legitimately serializes omitted list fields such as lifecycle `HostConfig.CapAdd` as `null`, while the validator called `.map()` directly. The crash masked the intended explicit topology-drift result and was independent of application workflow code or the database exchange.
- Capability, security-option, and tmpfs normalizers now treat absent/null Docker fields as empty values before comparison. Expected topology is still fail-closed: a missing required `CapDrop`, security option, mount, tmpfs entry, or other identity remains rejected by the existing explicit drift error. The source contract now guards these null-field cases.
- This is a CI evidence/diagnostic correction only; no product, schema, authorization, approval-routing, or persistent data behavior changed. Requested Code Spark and GPT-5.4-mini reviewers were unavailable; the closest permitted GPT-5.6 DevOps fallback reviewed the archive and correction. A fresh exact-SHA hosted run must pass both browser lanes, including desktop/mobile tests, secret scans, and teardown, before readiness credit advances. Phase I remains **77% local implementation / NO-GO** and Inventory Control Pilot remains **89% local implementation / NO-GO**.
- The same archive showed generated per-run authentication, encryption, SMTP, probe, and stop-token values in the Actions step environment summary because they were written through `GITHUB_ENV` without workflow masking. These values are ephemeral to the failed run, but exposing them is a release-evidence defect. The workflow now registers each generated secret with `::add-mask::` before writing the environment file. The existing artifact scans and cleanup gates remain unchanged; the next exact-SHA run must confirm masked logs and clean retained artifacts.
- The follow-up archives show the null crash is gone and secret masking works (`***`), but both lanes now fail closed at the intended `PRODUCTION_AUTH_E2E_PRIVATE_DB_LIFECYCLE_DRIFT` check. The validator previously reported only the aggregate code; it now includes a sanitized field list (`id`, `image`, `user`, `network_mode`, `readonly_rootfs`, `tmpfs`, `mounts`, etc.) so the next run identifies the exact Docker contract mismatch without exposing container metadata or secrets. No topology requirement is relaxed.
- Archive `logs_84613103894.zip` identifies the next admission defect: the ordinary `checks` job failed its source-contract assertion because the new null-safe access was written as `lifecycle?.HostConfig?.ReadonlyRootfs`, while the test required the obsolete `lifecycle.HostConfig?.ReadonlyRootfs` spelling. The browser lanes did not receive valid admission from that run. The contract now checks the corrected source shape; no runtime topology, product, authorization, or secret behavior changes.
- Archive `logs_84657529231` identifies one remaining stale source-contract assertion of the same class: the test still required `lifecycle.Config?.User`, while the null-safe validator uses `lifecycle?.Config?.User`. The runtime topology gate was not reached because `checks` stopped at this assertion. The contract is now aligned; no topology or product behavior changes.
- Archive `logs_84671970355` confirms the source-contract correction passed, but both admitted browser lanes now reach the runtime topology gate and fail closed with `PRODUCTION_AUTH_E2E_PRIVATE_DB_LIFECYCLE_DRIFT:tmpfs`. The lifecycle Compose service intentionally mounts bounded Vitest cache tmpfs paths at `/app/packages/database/node_modules/.vite` and `/app/apps/web/node_modules/.vite` in addition to `/tmp`; the runner's expected topology still listed only `/tmp`. The validator now expects all three declared tmpfs entries, and the contract pins those paths and sizes. This remains a CI topology-evidence correction; no product, schema, authorization, approval-routing, or persistent-data behavior changes.

### Hosted private-database image validation parity — August 7, 2026

- Exact candidate `2e72155` and GitHub Actions run [31145089836](https://github.com/tuklasdigitallabs/OGFI-ERPv2/actions/runs/31145089836) passed the prerequisite `checks` job, but both `production` and `bounded-uat` production-authenticated browser jobs failed before browser startup at `PRODUCTION_AUTH_E2E_PRIVATE_DB_MANIFEST_INVALID`.
- The failure is a runner-only validator drift. The shared private-database exchange accepts the immutable CI image form `docker.io/library/postgres:17-alpine@sha256:<64 hex>`, and the lifecycle produced that exact form. `scripts/production-auth-e2e-runner.mjs` retained a narrower duplicate expression that excluded `:` from the repository/tag portion, so it rejected the correctly pinned database image while accepting the bare lifecycle image. No browser, database, authorization, teardown, UAT, or release credit is claimed from this run.
- The runner expression now matches the shared validator, and its exchange regression uses the exact tagged-and-digest-pinned Postgres reference used by CI. Candidate/lifecycle images remain content-addressed and tag-only or malformed values remain rejected. This is a fail-closed CI evidence correction only; no product, schema, authorization authority, approval-routing flag, or persistent data changed.
- Requested Code Spark and GPT-5.4-mini reviewers were unavailable in the active toolset. The closest permitted GPT-5.6 DevOps and Security fallbacks reviewed the diagnosis and correction without relaxing any hard gate.
